登革热案件呈上升趋势;警惕严重并发症的风险。- 越南.vn

Dengue fever cases are on the rise; be vigilant for the risk of serious complications. - Vietnam.vn
新闻简报登革热在越南呈上升趋势,2026年前10个月报告病例超过170,684例,与2025年同期相比增加26%。南部地区占病例的66.8%,其次是中部高地、中部地区和北部。截至第 43 周末,胡志明市已有超过 42,500 例病例和 11 例死亡,而仅迪安区就报告了 861 例病例和 1 名儿童死亡。所有四种 登革病毒 类型(DENV-1、DENV-2、DENV-3 和 DENV-4)均在流通,其中 DENV-3 在多年不常录制后重新出现。这种疾病现在全年都会发生,而不仅仅是在雨季,专家警告说,再次感染可能会导致严重疾病。卫生部强调需要继续评估预防策略,并强调社区积极努力降低并发症和死亡风险。个人应密切监测症状,尤其是在患病的第三天至第七天,并避免自行用药。建议家庭清洁水容器并采取措施防止蚊虫叮咬,因为这些是 传播 的主要路线。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年前 10 个月,最新更新日期为 11/11/2026。
地点与对象
越南,重点关注南部、中部高地、中部地区和北部。胡志明市和迪安区被特别提及为有重大病例的地区。
事件
登革热病例数量持续上升,2026年前10个月报告病例超过170,000例,与2025年同期相比增加了26%。该疾病现在在许多地区全年发生,包括北境的一些山区省份。
规模、影响与风险
2026年前10个月报告了170,684例病例和21例死亡。南部地区占总病例数的66.8%,其次是中部高地地区(17.5%)、中部地区(9.8%)和北部地区(5.9%)。截至第 43 周末,胡志明市已有超过 42,500 例病例和 11 例死亡。仅迪安区就有 861 例病例和 1 名儿童死亡。由于所有四种 登革病毒 类型的循环,再感染和严重并发症的风险仍然很高。
应对、状态与不确定性
专家强调,需要积极预防和社区持续努力,因为仅仅喷洒杀虫剂是不够的。监测结果显示了病毒类型的分布,对怀疑患有监测的个体应进行密切检查和监测。卫生部正在评估现有的预防措施并更新科学方法。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dengue fever cases are on the rise; be vigilant for the risk of serious complications. - Vietnam.vn

News Brief
Dengue fever is on the rise in Vietnam, with over 170,684 cases reported in the first 10 months of 2026, a 26% increase compared to the same period in 2025. The South region accounted for 66.8% of these cases, followed by the Central Highlands, Central region, and North. In Ho Chi Minh City, more than 42,500 cases and 11 deaths were recorded by the end of week 43, while Di An ward alone reported 861 cases and one child death. All four dengue virus types—DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3, and DENV-4—are circulating, with DENV-3 reappearing after years of infrequent recording. The disease is now occurring year-round, not just during the rainy season, and experts warn that reinfection can lead to severe illness. The Ministry of Health highlights the need for continued evaluation of prevention strategies and emphasizes proactive community efforts to reduce the risk of complications and deaths. Individuals should monitor symptoms closely, especially during the third to seventh day of illness, and avoid self-medication. Families are advised to clean water containers and take measures to prevent mosquito bites, as these are the primary transmission routes.
Original source text (English)
English Login Homepage Topic News Political System Destination Event Travel Happy Vietnam Enterprise Product Heritage Museum Figure Multimedia Data Home Culture – Society Y tế Dengue fever cases are on the rise; be vigilant for the risk of serious complications. In the first 10 months of 2026, the whole country recorded more than 170,000 cases of dengue fever, a 26% increase compared to the same period last year. Notably, all four types of dengue virus are circulating in many localities. Experts advise people not to be complacent about the initial signs and to proactively prevent the disease to reduce the risk of complications and death. Báo Phú Thọ•11/11/2026 Follow Vietnam.vn on Google Add Vietnam.vn on Google 0 The number of cases is increasing, and the course of the epidemic is unpredictable. According to the Ministry of Health , in the first 10 months of 2026, the whole country recorded 170,684 cases of dengue fever and 21 deaths, an increase of 26% compared to the same period in 2025. The South accounted for 66.8% of the total cases, followed by the Central Highlands at 17.5%, the Central region at 9.8%, and the North at 5.9%. In Ho Chi Minh City, by the end of week 43, the city had recorded over 42,500 cases of dengue fever, including 11 deaths. Di An ward alone accounted for 861 cases and one child death. Khám phá thêm Đọc Mẹo Du Lịch Khám phá ĐNA Đọc Tin Tức Notably, the epidemiological characteristics of dengue fever are changing. While previously the disease typically increased sharply during the rainy season, mainly concentrated in the South and Central regions, dengue fever can now occur year-round in many localities, including some mountainous provinces in the North such as Tuyen Quang and Son La . Along with the expansion of its distribution area, all four dengue virus types, DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3, and DENV-4, have been recorded in Vietnam. Of these, DENV-3 reappeared from the end of 2024 after many years of infrequent recording. Surveillance results in the southern region in September 2026 showed that DENV-2 accounted for 69% and DENV-1 accounted for 27.5% of the samples with identified virus types. According to experts, people who have previously had dengue fever can still be reinfected because the immunity acquired after the illness does not provide adequate protection against other types of the virus. In some cases, reinfection can even lead to severe illness. Dr. Hoang Minh Duc, Director of the Department of Disease Prevention (Ministry of Health), stated that despite Vietnam's implementation of numerous prevention and control measures, dengue fever remains an endemic disease with a high incidence rate. This reality necessitates continued evaluation of the effectiveness of existing solutions, updating scientific advancements, and selecting disease prevention methods appropriate to practical conditions. Tìm hiểu lịch sử You may also like Not only is dengue fever increasing in number, but it is also concerning because of its potential for severe complications and damage to multiple organs. The disease often begins with symptoms such as high fever, headache, eye pain, muscle and joint pain, or nausea, which can easily be mistaken for common viral infections. According to Professor Nguyen Van Kinh, former Director of the Central Hospital for Tropical Diseases, the period from the third to the seventh day of the illness requires special monitoring. When the patient's fever begins to subside, the risk of plasma leakage, shock, and serious complications can still occur. Young children, the elderly, and individuals with underlying conditions such as diabetes, hypertension, cardiovascular disease, kidney disease, or obesity require close monitoring due to their higher risk of complications. Take proactive measures to prevent disease, don't wait for an outbreak to occur. Given the unpredictable nature of dengue fever, experts emphasize the need to shift from responding to outbreaks when they occur to proactive, ongoing prevention efforts within the community. Dengue fever is primarily transmitted through the Aedes aegypti mosquito. The mosquito bites an infected person and can then transmit the disease to a healthy person through subsequent bites. Notably, Aedes mosquitoes can breed even in small water containers around the house. Therefore, simply spraying insecticides when an outbreak occurs is not enough to control the risk of transmission. Families should regularly clean and cover water containers, remove items that can collect water, collect waste, and dispose of breeding grounds. At the same time, they should proactively prevent mosquito bites using appropriate measures such as using mosquito nets, wearing long-sleeved clothing, or using mosquito repellent products as directed, even during the day. You may also like Along with controlling disease-carrying mosquitoes, early detection and proper monitoring of the disease's progression are crucial in limiting complications. Individuals suspected of having dengue fever should be examined, have their risk assessed, and follow the instructions of healthcare professionals, especially during the stage when the disease may become severe. Symptoms such as severe abdominal pain, persistent vomiting, unusual bleeding, lethargy, extreme fatigue, or cold hands and feet require urgent medical evaluation. Patients should not rely solely on the reduction of fever to conclude that their condition is improving, and should avoid self-medicating with inappropriate drugs. With all four types of Dengue virus circulating, the risk of reinfection remains. Vaccination is a preventive measure that should be advised based on age, health status, and professional guidance, and must be combined with measures to prevent mosquito bites. https://daidoanket.vn/chuyen-muc/suc-khoe.html According to daidoanket.vn Source: Báo Phú Thọ Original articleCopy https://baophutho.vn/sot-xuat-huyet-gia-tang-canh-giac-nguy-co-bien-chung-nang-264205.htm Follow Vietnam.vn on Google Add Vietnam.vn on Google 0 Tag: Riskdeathrecorded#Contracted dengue fevervirusdisease preventionCentral Hospital for Tropical DiseasescomplicationscirculateChuyên gia Comment (0) Top Interests Newest Please leave a comment to share your feelings! Vietnam, with a focus on the South, Central Highlands, Central region, and North. Ho Chi Minh City and Di An ward are specifically mentioned as areas with significant cases.
Event
Dengue fever cases are increasing, with over 170,000 cases reported in the first 10 months of 2026, a 26% rise compared to the same period in 2025. The disease is now occurring year-round in many localities, including some mountainous provinces in the North.
Scale, impact and risk
170,684 cases and 21 deaths were reported in the first 10 months of 2026. The South accounted for 66.8% of the total cases, followed by the Central Highlands at 17.5%, the Central region at 9.8%, and the North at 5.9%. In Ho Chi Minh City, over 42,500 cases and 11 deaths were recorded by the end of week 43. Di An ward alone had 861 cases and one child death. The risk of reinfection and severe complications remains high due to the circulation of all four dengue virus types.
Response, status and uncertainty
Experts emphasize the need for proactive prevention and ongoing community efforts, as simply spraying insecticides is not enough. Surveillance results show the distribution of virus types, and individuals suspected of having dengue fever should be examined and monitored closely. The Ministry of Health is evaluating existing prevention measures and updating scientific approaches.

Dengue Virus Evades Innate Antiviral Defenses as Dysregulated Death Pathways Drive Severe Pathology - Bioengineer.org

This article, published on November 11, 2026, presents a narrative review of dengue virus literature from 2000 to 2026, emphasizing that disease severity is primarily driven by host immune reactions rather than viral replication alone. The dengue virus, an RNA pathogen in the Flaviviridae family, comprises four serotypes (DENV-1 to DENV-4) and is transmitted by Aedes mosquitoes in tropical and subtropical regions, infecting an estimated 3.6 billion people across over 100 countries. Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Thursday, November 12, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Dengue Virus Evades Innate Antiviral Defenses as Dysregulated Death Pathways Drive Severe Pathology by November 11, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Dengue Virus Evades Innate Antiviral Defenses as Dysregulated Death Pathways Drive Severe Pathology Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A narrative review examining dengue virus literature published between 2000 and 2026 establishes that disease severity is driven primarily by host immune reactions rather than viral replication alone. Dengue virus, an RNA pathogen belonging to the genus Flavivirus within the family Flaviviridae, comprises four antigenically distinct serotypes, DENV-1 through DENV-4, differing by twenty-five to thirty percent at the amino acid level. Primarily transmitted by Aedes aegypti and Aedes albopictus mosquitoes across tropical and subtropical regions, the virus infects an estimated 3.6 billion people in over one hundred countries, causing hundreds of millions of annual infections. Inoculation into host skin initiates infection before the virus disseminates through the lymphatic system and bloodstream to infect the spleen and liver. While clinical manifestations range from asymptomatic infection and classic dengue fever to severe hemorrhagic fever and shock syndrome, review findings demonstrate that life-threatening plasma leakage and hemorrhage stem from critically dysregulated innate immune pathways and uncontrolled inflammatory responses. Innate immune defense begins with pattern-recognition receptors detecting conserved pathogen-associated molecular patterns to trigger type I interferons and pro-inflammatory cytokines. Endosomal Toll-like receptors execute essential nucleic acid surveillance: Toll-like receptor 3 recognizes viral double-stranded RNA replication intermediates, whereas Toll-like receptor 7 detects single-stranded RNA rich in guanosine and uridine. Predominantly expressed in dendritic cells, monocytes, and macrophages, these receptors engage specialized intracellular adapters upon ligand binding. Toll-like receptor 3 signals through TIR-domain-containing adapter inducing interferon-β, while Toll-like receptor 7 relies on myeloid differentiation primary response 88. These adapters recruit tumor necrosis factor receptor-associated factors, activating TANK-binding kinase 1 and IκB kinase complexes. Phosphorylation of interferon regulatory factors 3 and 7 alongside nuclear factor-κB induces type I interferons and cytokines such as interleukin-6 and tumor necrosis factor-α. Subsequent interferon binding to cell-surface receptors stimulates JAK-STAT signaling, phosphorylating STAT1 and STAT2 to induce interferon-stimulated genes, although excessive pathway activation fosters severe immunopathology. Within the cytosolic compartment, the RIG-I-like receptor family serves as a frontline surveillance network for sensing dengue viral RNA. This pathway utilizes two key sensors: retinoic acid-inducible gene I, which detects short double-stranded or single-stranded viral RNA bearing 5′-triphosphate or 5′-diphosphate groups, and melanoma differentiation-associated protein 5, which recognizes long double-stranded RNA. Ligand binding prompts conformational shifts exposing caspase activation and recruitment domains, which bind mitochondrial antiviral signaling protein on the outer mitochondrial membrane. Mitochondrial antiviral signaling protein subsequently functions as an organizing platform to recruit tumor necrosis factor receptor-associated factors, TANK-binding kinase 1, and IκB kinase-ε. These kinases phosphorylate interferon regulatory factors 3 and 7, prompting dimerization and nuclear translocation, while concurrently stimulating nuclear factor-κB. This concerted transcription produces type I interferons that act in autocrine and paracrine loops through interferon receptors, driving JAK-STAT signaling and interferon-stimulated gene synthesis to establish an antiviral cellular state. Discover more Engineering job board Research publication platform Scientific instrument sales Laboratory equipment supplier Biotech startup funding Although dengue virus possesses an RNA genome that does not directly bind cytosolic DNA sensors, the cyclic GMP-AMP synthase and stimulator of interferon genes pathway contributes indirectly to antiviral defense. Cellular stress and mitochondrial damage induced by infection trigger the release of host mitochondrial DNA into the cytosol. Cytosolic DNA activates cyclic GMP-AMP synthase to generate the second messenger 2′3′-cyclic GMP-AMP, which binds stimulator of interferon genes localized in the endoplasmic reticulum. Once activated, stimulator of interferon genes recruits TANK-binding kinase 1 to phosphorylate interferon regulatory factor 3, stimulating type I interferon expression alongside nuclear factor-κB-dependent pro-inflammatory cytokines. However, the review emphasizes that this indirect DNA-sensing response varies considerably across experimental cell types and infection conditions. Consequently, evidence derived from laboratory models regarding the protective magnitude of the cyclic GMP-AMP synthase axis must be interpreted cautiously before extrapolating findings directly to human dengue pathogenesis. Dengue virus deploys nonstructural proteins to systematically counter host innate signaling at multiple biochemical steps. Nonstructural protein NS3 binds the chaperone 14-3-3ε to block retinoic acid-inducible gene I translocation, while NS4A disrupts its association with mitochondrial antiviral signaling protein, particularly in serotype 1. Furthermore, nonstructural proteins NS2A and NS4B inhibit TANK-binding kinase 1 activation and interferon regulatory factor 3 phosphorylation across serotypes 1, 2, and 4. The pathogen also targets cytosolic DNA sensing: NS2B promotes cyclic GMP-AMP synthase degradation via an autophagy-lysosomal pathway, and the NS2B/3 protease cleaves human stimulator of interferon genes to disrupt downstream antiviral transcription. Downstream of interferon reception, nonstructural proteins NS2A, NS4A, and NS4B inhibit STAT1 phosphorylation, while NS5 binds STAT2 to trigger its proteasomal degradation. By dismantling the STAT1-STAT2-IRF9 complex, the virus suppresses interferon-stimulated gene transcription, enabling viral persistence and promoting disease progression. Cell death receptor signaling provides a delicate equilibrium between eliminating infected cells and causing host tissue damage. Ligation of surface death receptors, including Fas, tumor necrosis factor receptor 1, and TRAIL receptors, initiates extrinsic apoptosis through the death-inducing signaling complex containing Fas-associated death domain protein and procaspase-8. Cleaved caspase-8 activates executioner caspases 3 and 7 to execute cellular death. In early infection, apoptosis restricts viral amplification by destroying infected host cells; however, excessive receptor activation precipitates severe complications. Clinical data link increased disease severity to elevated circulating levels of tumor necrosis factor-α, Fas ligand, and TRAIL. Unrestrained apoptosis of vascular endothelial cells disrupts barrier integrity, causing plasma leakage and vascular shock. Similarly, widespread lymphocyte apoptosis impairs adaptive immunity. Meanwhile, nuclear factor-κB signaling can counteract apoptosis by transcribing antiapoptotic genes, including BCL-2, BCL-XL, and XIAP, generating a fragile balance between cell survival and inflammatory tissue destruction. Accurate pathology assessment requires distinguishing apoptosis from alternative regulated cell death modes, specifically necroptosis and pyroptosis. Apoptosis depends on caspases and maintains plasma membrane integrity early, restricting intracellular content release. In contrast, necroptosis is a lytic, pro-inflammatory process driven by receptor-interacting protein kinase 1, receptor-interacting protein kinase 3, and mixed-lineage kinase domain-like protein. Under normal conditions, caspase-8 prevents necroptosis by cleaving receptor-interacting protein kinases 1 and 3; when caspase-8 is suppressed, necroptosis ensues. Pyroptosis represents another distinct inflammatory death mechanism, mediated by inflammatory caspases and gasdermin proteins that form plasma membrane pores to release inflammatory mediators. The review emphasizes that although these cell death pathways intersect, they are mechanistically separate. Assertions that necroptosis or pyroptosis drive dengue pathology require explicit biochemical confirmation, such as mixed-lineage kinase domain-like protein activation or gasdermin cleavage, rather than theoretical assumptions drawn from unrelated viral infections. Translating innate immune concepts into dengue vaccines highlights major challenges regarding safety and serotype-specific protection, as demonstrated by licensed vaccines CYD-TDV and TAK-003. CYD-TDV, a live-attenuated chimeric vaccine on a yellow fever 17D backbone given in three doses over twelve months, increased the risk of severe disease in seronegative recipients, requiring pre-vaccination screening before its manufacturer discontinued production due to low demand. Conversely, TAK-003 is a live-attenuated tetravalent vaccine with a DENV-2 backbone given in two doses three months apart, recommended by the World Health Organization for children aged six to sixteen in high-transmission areas. However, protection varies by serotype and baseline serostatus. Developing future vaccines requires balanced, durable tetravalent protection without provoking antibody-dependent enhancement. Adjuvants targeting innate pathways, including Toll-like receptor agonists, are under evaluation to enhance antigen presentation, though candidate formulations require rigorous safety testing to prevent excessive inflammation. Therapeutic strategies under investigation target both viral enzymatic machinery and host immune pathways to curb viral replication and inflammation. The viral NS2B/3 protease represents a prominent target because selective inhibitors could simultaneously block viral polyprotein processing and protect human stimulator of interferon genes from cleavage. Nevertheless, NS2B/3 inhibitors remain experimental, requiring improvements in selectivity, intracellular potency, and post-symptomatic efficacy. Host-directed interventions targeting innate pathways, including STING agonists such as cAIMP and diABZI or investigational Toll-like receptor agonists, stimulate protective type I interferons but pose risks of severe cytokine release and tissue toxicity. Similarly, exogenous interferon therapy faces administration timing constraints. Interventions modulating cell death, such as pan-caspase inhibitor z-VAD-FMK, BCL-2 inhibitor venetoclax, inhibitor of apoptosis protein antagonist xevinapant, and TRAIL receptor agonists, remain in preclinical stages. Because untargeted apoptotic modulation could destroy healthy tissue or hinder viral clearance, none has demonstrated clinical efficacy in dengue. Critical research gaps continue to hinder the translation of innate immune and apoptotic pathway modulators into approved clinical therapies for dengue. Detailed mechanisms explaining how viral nonstructural proteins differentially affect pattern-recognition signaling across immune and vascular endothelial cells remain incompletely defined. Additionally, the precise biochemical threshold separating protective antiviral defense from destructive immunopathology remains unresolved, as does the role of cell death in promoting vascular leakage. Existing research relies heavily on in vitro and animal models that fail to capture human disease dynamics, underscoring the need for organoid platforms and multi-omics profiling to identify reliable severity biomarkers. Because no targeted therapeutic has proven effective enough to replace supportive fluid therapy, future clinical success will depend on identifying suitable therapeutic windows and developing combination regimens that couple direct-acting antivirals with host-directed modulators to control viral replication without triggering tissue damage. B routine priorityPublic-health news

With dengue fever cases exceeding 170,000, we cannot rely solely on mosquito control. - Vietnam.vn

News Brief
Vietnam is facing a significant dengue fever outbreak, with over 170,000 cases and 21 deaths reported in the first 10 months of 2026. The disease is no longer following a clear seasonal pattern and is spreading to new regions, including previously low-risk mountainous areas. Experts at the Dengue Fever Conference 2026, held from November 6-11, warned that current prevention and control efforts are insufficient. They emphasized the need for a proactive approach, combining vaccination, mosquito eradication, and early detection to manage the disease effectively. The conference also highlighted the challenges posed by the circulation of all four dengue virus serotypes (DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3, DENV-4), climate change, and mosquito resistance to insecticides. While no specific drug exists for dengue fever, treatment focuses on managing symptoms and preventing complications. The Vietnamese health sector is working to implement these strategies, but the unpredictable nature of the disease continues to pose a challenge.
Original source text (English)
English Login Homepage Topic News Political System Destination Event Travel Happy Vietnam Enterprise Product Heritage Museum Figure Multimedia Data Home Culture – Society Y tế With dengue fever cases exceeding 170,000, we cannot rely solely on mosquito control. Dengue fever is becoming increasingly complex, with over 170,000 cases and 21 deaths in the first 10 months of 2026. Worryingly, the disease no longer follows a clear seasonal pattern, and its distribution areas are expanding. This reality demands a new approach and a strengthening of proactive prevention measures. Check Symptoms Hà Nội Mới•06/11/2026 Follow Vietnam.vn on Google Add Vietnam.vn on Google 0 Hanoi is disseminating information on dengue fever prevention and control measures to households. Photo: Hanoi Department of Health . 21 deaths in 10 months At the Dengue Fever Conference 2026, held on November 6-11 and chaired by the Department of Disease Prevention ( Ministry of Health ) in collaboration with many other units, experts warned that dengue fever continues to be a worrying health burden in Vietnam. Check Symptoms Discover more Check Symptoms Find Treatments Buy Bug Kits According to information released at the conference, in the first 10 months of 2026, the whole country recorded 170,684 cases of dengue fever (a 1.3-fold increase compared to the same period in 2025), including 21 deaths. Notably, the disease is endemic year-round in all provinces and cities and is spreading to even mountainous areas in the North such as Tuyen Quang and Son La - areas that previously recorded few cases. Dr. Hoang Minh Duc, Director of the Department of Disease Prevention (Ministry of Health), speaks at the conference. Photo: Organizing Committee. The expansion of dengue fever's prevalence and the increasing number of cases indicate that dengue prevention and control efforts are facing numerous challenges. Speaking at the conference, Dr. Hoang Minh Duc, Director of the Department of Disease Prevention (Ministry of Health), stated that despite the health sector implementing many measures, mobilizing resources from the central to local levels, and coordinating with domestic and international experts, disease control has not yet achieved sustainable effectiveness. Check Symptoms One factor complicating the dengue fever situation is the circulation of all four dengue virus serotypes: DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3, and DENV-4 in Vietnam. The prevalence of these serotypes varies over time and across regions. The movement of serotypes, combined with climate change, rapid urbanization, and mosquito resistance to insecticides, makes predicting and controlling outbreaks increasingly difficult. You may also like People's Physician - Professor Dr. Nguyen Van Kinh, former Director of the Central Hospital for Tropical Diseases , believes that the increasing trend in dengue fever cases, which no longer follows a clear seasonal or cyclical pattern as before, is partly due to climate change and increased rainfall, creating favorable conditions for disease-transmitting mosquitoes to breed and develop. Find Treatments According to Professor Nguyen Van Kinh, although the mortality rate recorded in Vietnam is currently low (about 0.01%), dengue fever remains a disease with complex and unpredictable clinical course. The severity varies from person to person, depending on immune response, age, immune status, and underlying medical conditions. In particular, more than 90% of people infected with the Dengue virus are asymptomatic but can still participate in the disease transmission cycle through mosquitoes carrying the virus. Regarding treatment, Professor Nguyen Van Kinh stated that there is currently no specific drug for dengue fever, and treatment mainly focuses on managing symptoms, controlling shock, and preventing severe complications. During outbreaks, the overcrowding in hospitals continues to put significant pressure on the medical staff. Expanding proactive disease prevention strategies. Given the increasingly unpredictable nature of dengue fever, focusing solely on controlling outbreaks or eradicating mosquitoes when cases increase is insufficient. Experts believe that a strong shift towards proactive prevention is needed, combining multiple solutions to reduce the risk of infection within the community. Vaccination is considered an additional preventive tool alongside measures to control disease-carrying mosquitoes. Check Symptoms Conference scene. Photo: Organizing Committee At the conference, experiences in implementing vaccination programs in several countries provided further practical evidence of the role of vaccines in disease prevention and control. Dr. Rosana Richtmann, Director of the Infection Control Committee (ICC) at Santa Joana Obstetrics and Gynecology Hospital and former President of the Infectious Diseases Society of São Paulo State ( Brazil ), stated that Brazil has included the dengue fever vaccine in its public immunization program since 2024. The program was initially implemented in 521 localities, then expanded to 2,752 localities, with a total of 24 million doses of vaccine distributed. Notably, actual data from São Paulo state in 2024-2025 showed that receiving two doses reduced the risk of symptomatic dengue fever by 73.1% and the risk of hospitalization by 87.9%. The protective effect is maintained even if the DENV-3 virus type reappears. You may also like Not only in Brazil, but also in Indonesia , vaccines are being deployed in combination with medical technology applications to enhance proactive prevention in high-risk areas. In Vietnam, the dengue fever vaccine currently in circulation is the first and only one approved by the Ministry of Health. The vaccine has achieved pre-qualification by the World Health Organization (WHO) for quality, safety, and efficacy, and is recommended by the WHO for use in countries with a high burden of dengue fever. To date, the vaccine has been approved in 43 countries, with over 32 million doses distributed globally; it has also been implemented in public immunization programs in Brazil, Argentina, Colombia, Indonesia, Honduras, Paraguay, Peru, and Thailand. To ensure the effectiveness of dengue prevention and control strategies, experts once again emphasized the need for the simultaneous implementation of three groups of solutions: vaccination to prevent the disease; mosquito eradication and elimination of larvae to reduce the risk of transmission; and early detection and timely treatment to limit complications and mortality. Each solution plays a distinct role but is closely related and complements the others. Combining these measures regularly and continuously will contribute to controlling the epidemic and reducing the burden of dengue fever in the community. 登革热病例超过170,000例,专家警告仅靠灭蚊不足以应对 - Vietnam.vn

With dengue fever cases exceeding 170,000, experts warn that mosquito eradication alone is not enough. - Vietnam.vn
新闻简报2026年11月,越南报告了超过170,000例登革热病例,其中21例死亡,凸显了该疾病日益严峻的挑战。2026年11月6日举行的登革热科学会议汇聚了700多名国内外代表,包括世界卫生组织(WHO)和卫生部的代表。专家警告称,仅靠消灭蚊子不足以应对暴发,该疾病已传播至如宣光和 Sơn La 等山区地区。目前,该病毒的四种血清型(DENV-1、DENV-2、DENV-3、DENV-4)均在该国流行。会议强调需要采取多管齐下的方法,结合疫苗接种、蚊虫控制和公众意识提升。积极的预防措施至关重要,因为该疾病由于气候变化、城市化和抗药性蚊虫而变得更加难以预测。会议还讨论了登革热诊断和治疗的草案指南,强调了在管理疫情中采用基于证据的策略的重要性。

越南,病例蔓延至宣光省和山罗省等北部山区(N13)。

越南登革热病例超过170,000例,专家警告仅靠灭蚊不足以应对(N1,N2,N10)
规模、影响与风险
2026 年前 10 个月报告了 170,684 例病例和 21 例死亡(N2、N10)。疫情已向山区蔓延,目前所有四种血清型(DENV-1、DENV-2、DENV-3、DENV-4)均在流行(N14)。
应对、状态与不确定性
专家强调需要采取积极的预防措施,包括疫苗接种、蚊虫控制和流行病学监测(N3,N17,N19,N28)。会议吸引了700多名代表,包括WHO和卫生部的代表(N4,N11)
B routine priorityPublic-health news

With dengue fever cases exceeding 170,000, experts warn that mosquito eradication alone is not enough. - Vietnam.vn

News Brief
In November 2026, Vietnam reported over 170,000 dengue fever cases, with 21 deaths, highlighting the growing challenge of the disease. The 2026 Dengue Fever Scientific Conference, held on November 6th, brought together over 700 domestic and international delegates, including representatives from the World Health Organization (WHO) and the Ministry of Health. Experts warned that mosquito eradication alone is not enough to combat the outbreak, which has spread to mountainous regions like Tuyen Quang and Son La. All four serotypes of the dengue virus (DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3, DENV-4) are currently circulating in the country. The conference emphasized the need for a multi-pronged approach, combining vaccination, mosquito control, and public awareness. Proactive prevention measures are crucial, as the disease has become more unpredictable due to climate change, urbanization, and insecticide-resistant mosquitoes. The draft guidelines for dengue diagnosis and treatment were also discussed, underscoring the importance of evidence-based strategies in managing the epidemic.

(CLO) In the first 10 months of 2026, Vietnam recorded 170,684 cases of dengue fever, with 21 deaths. Given the increasingly unpredictable nature of the epidemic, experts emphasize the need to shift strongly towards proactive prevention, combining mosquito control, vaccination, and epidemiological surveillance. Check Symptoms Công Luận•06/11/2026 Follow Vietnam.vn on Google Add Vietnam.vn on Google 0 On November 6th, the 2026 Dengue Fever Scientific Conference officially commenced, attracting over 700 domestic and international delegates, including representatives from the World Health Organization (WHO), the Ministry of Health, and health sector leaders from 34 provinces and cities. 170,000 cases, the epidemic has spread to mountainous areas. According to figures shared at the conference, dengue fever is currently one of the fastest-growing mosquito-borne infectious diseases in the world , with approximately 100-400 million cases annually. In Vietnam, this disease has created a long-standing healthcare burden for more than four decades. From 1980 to the present, the country has recorded hundreds of thousands of cases each year, and there have been deaths every year. Discover more Buy Bug Kits Book Pest Control Find Treatments Notably, in 2025, Vietnam recorded 184,903 cases. In the first 10 months of 2026, this number continued to be high with 170,684 cases, including 21 deaths. Tìm hiểu lịch sử On November 6th, the Dengue Fever Conference 2026 brought together over 700 domestic and international delegates, along with representatives from the Ministry of Health, WHO, and the health sectors of 34 provinces and cities. Photo: Nguyen Thi Not only has the number of cases increased, but the distribution of the disease has also undergone significant changes. Dengue fever now occurs year-round in provinces and cities, spreading to mountainous areas in the North such as Tuyen Quang and Son La , which previously recorded few cases. Check Symptoms All four serotypes of the Dengue virus (DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3, DENV-4) are currently circulating in Vietnam. Changes in the dominant serotype over time and across regions, combined with climate change, rapid urbanization, and the emergence of insecticide-resistant mosquitoes, are making disease forecasting and control increasingly complex. You may also like In light of this situation, Dr. Hoang Minh Duc, Director of the Department of Disease Prevention (Ministry of Health), believes that decisive, proactive, and evidence- based disease prevention measures need to be implemented. Referencing and applying international experience adapted to Vietnam's conditions, combined with controlling disease-transmitting mosquitoes and raising public awareness, is crucial in reducing the burden of dengue fever. Check Symptoms Vaccination is incorporated into the overall disease prevention strategy. One of the notable topics at the conference was the practical evidence of the effectiveness of vaccination in preventing dengue fever in several countries. Dr. Rosana Richtmann, Director of the Infection Control Committee at Santa Joana Obstetrics and Gynecology Hospital and former President of the São Paulo Infectious Diseases Society ( Brazil ), stated that Brazil began implementing the dengue fever vaccine in its public immunization program in 2024, initially in 521 localities, and later expanding to 2,752 localities, with 24 million doses distributed. Data from São Paulo in 2024-2025 showed that those who received both doses had a 73.1% reduced risk of symptomatic dengue fever and an 87.9% reduced risk of hospitalization. This protective effect was observed even if the DENV-3 virus type reappeared. Check Symptoms In Indonesia, vaccination is also implemented in conjunction with the application of medical technology to enhance prevention in high-risk areas. Prof. Dr. Nguyen Van Kinh, former Director of the Central Hospital for Tropical Diseases. Photo: Nguyen Thi In Vietnam, one topic of interest is the updates in the draft Guidelines for the Diagnosis and Treatment of Dengue Fever in 2026, presented by People's Physician, Professor, Doctor Nguyen Van Kinh, former Director of the National Hospital for Tropical Diseases. Check Symptoms You may also like Accordingly, the prevention section emphasizes the role of proactive measures, especially in the context of the current lack of a specific treatment for dengue fever. Recommended solutions include vaccination according to current regulations, monitoring and prevention according to the Ministry of Health's guidelines, and controlling disease-transmitting insects by avoiding mosquito bites and eliminating mosquito larvae. This multi-pronged approach aligns with the WHO's direction for an integrated dengue control strategy, rather than focusing on a single solution. To translate scientific evidence into practice, collaboration between regulatory bodies, healthcare professionals, and partners is identified as crucial. Mr. Benjamin Ping, General Director of Takeda Vietnam, stated that although awareness of dengue fever in Vietnam is relatively high, many people still do not fully understand the risks and severity of the disease for themselves and their families, as well as the necessity of proactive prevention. According to him, it is necessary to strengthen evidence-based preventive measures, with vaccination being an integral part of the overall disease prevention and control strategy. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

超过 170,000 个 登革热 病例,需要大力转向主动预防。- 越南.vn

Over 170,000 dengue fever cases, requiring a strong shift towards proactive prevention. - Vietnam.vn
新闻简报越南在2026年前10个月报告了超过170,000例 登革热 病例,比2025年同期增长了26%。于11月6日在河内举行的 登革热 2026 会议强调需要更强有力地转向主动预防策略。该疾病现已在许多地区全年地方性流行,包括此前低风险的北部山区省份。会议强调结合 疫苗接种、蚊虫控制和流行病学 监测,以降低 登革热 的负担。更新的 诊断 和 治疗 指南已发布,纳入了新的 WHO 建议和实践经验。监测 数据显示,优势血清型为 DENV-2 和 DENV-1,DENV-3 在长时间缺失后再次出现。专家强调,虽然 疫苗接种 能降低登革热症状出现和住院的风险,但必须与其他措施结合使用,因为目前不存在特定的 抗病毒药物 治疗。病例上升和流行病学模式的变化凸显了持续评估和调整预防策略的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026年前10个月,会议于2026年11月6日举行。
地点与对象
越南,病例分布在南部(66.8%)、中部高地(17.5%)、中部地区(9.8%)和北部(5.9%),影响儿童、老年人和有基础健康问题的人。
事件
Vietnam recorded over 170,000 dengue fever cases in the first 10 months of 2026, a 26% increase compared to the same period in 2025, as reported by the Dengue Fever Conference 2026 and the Department of Disease Prevention.
规模、影响与风险
报告了 170,684 例病例和 21 例死亡,该疾病对疾病控制提出了越来越大的挑战,需要对严重症状进行特别监测,特别是在发病的第三天至第七天。
应对、状态与不确定性
会议讨论了转向主动预防的必要性,将疫苗接种、蚊虫控制和流行病学监测相结合。疾病预防部门强调了这些措施的重要性,并为诊断和治疗提出了更新的指南。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Over 170,000 dengue fever cases, requiring a strong shift towards proactive prevention. - Vietnam.vn

News Brief
Vietnam has reported over 170,000 cases of dengue fever in the first 10 months of 2026, marking a 26% increase compared to the same period in 2025. The Dengue Fever Conference 2026, held on November 6th in Hanoi, highlighted the need for a stronger shift towards proactive prevention strategies. The disease is now endemic year-round in many regions, including previously low-risk mountainous provinces in the North. The conference emphasized the importance of combining vaccination, mosquito control, and epidemiological surveillance to reduce the burden of dengue fever. Updated guidelines for diagnosis and treatment were presented, incorporating new WHO recommendations and practical experience. The dominant serotypes, DENV-2 and DENV-1, were identified in surveillance data, with DENV-3 reappearing after a long absence. Experts stressed that while vaccination can reduce the risk of symptomatic dengue and hospitalization, it must be integrated with other measures, as no specific antiviral treatment exists. The rising cases and changing epidemiological patterns underscore the need for continued evaluation and adaptation of prevention strategies.
Original source text (English)
English Login Homepage Topic News Political System Destination Event Travel Happy Vietnam Enterprise Product Heritage Museum Figure Multimedia Data Home Culture – Society Y tế Over 170,000 dengue fever cases, requiring a strong shift towards proactive prevention. In the first 10 months of 2026, Vietnam recorded more than 170,000 cases of dengue fever, a 26% increase compared to the same period last year. Tìm hiểu lịch sử Báo Đầu tư•06/11/2026 Follow Vietnam.vn on Google Add Vietnam.vn on Google 0 Given the increasingly complex nature of the epidemic, experts emphasize the need for a change in approach, combining epidemiological surveillance, control of disease-carrying mosquitoes, and vaccination to reduce the risk of illness, hospitalization, and death. Dengue fever is on the rise, posing an increasing challenge to disease control. On November 6th, in Hanoi, the Dengue Fever Conference 2026 brought together over 700 delegates and experts from Vietnam and abroad, along with representatives from the Ministry of Health , the World Health Organization (WHO), and the health sectors of 34 provinces and cities. The conference focused on assessing the epidemiological situation, updating scientific evidence, treatment experiences, and preventive measures against dengue fever, given that the disease continues to place a significant burden on the healthcare system. According to Dr. Hoang Minh Duc, Director of the Department of Disease Prevention (Ministry of Health ), despite Vietnam's implementation of numerous prevention and control measures, dengue fever remains an endemic infectious disease with a high number of cases. Information presented at the conference showed that in the first 10 months of 2026, the whole country recorded 170,684 cases of dengue fever and 21 deaths, an increase of 26% compared to the same period in 2025. The South accounted for 66.8% of the total cases, followed by the Central Highlands at 17.5%, the Central region at 9.8%, and the North at 5.9%. Check Symptoms Previously, in 2025, Vietnam recorded 184,903 cases of dengue fever. These figures continue to reflect the persistent burden of dengue fever, a disease that has been endemic in Vietnam for more than four decades and still records annual deaths. Notably, the epidemiological characteristics are undergoing significant changes. While previously dengue fever typically increased during the rainy season and was concentrated mainly in the South and Central regions, it is now endemic year-round in many localities, including mountainous provinces in the North such as Tuyen Quang and Son La , which previously recorded few cases. With the expansion of its distribution area, all four dengue virus serotypes—DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3, and DENV-4—are now present in Vietnam. The dominant serotype changes over time and across regions, with DENV-3 reappearing since late 2024 after many years of infrequent cases. Check Symptoms Surveillance data from the southern region in September 2026 showed that DENV-2 accounted for 69% and DENV-1 for 27.5% of samples with identified viral types. Because post-infection immunity does not provide full protection against other types, people who have had dengue fever are still at risk of reinfection, and may even experience severe symptoms in subsequent infections. Besides the simultaneous circulation of multiple virus types, climate change, erratic weather, rapid urbanization, and the emergence of insecticide-resistant mosquitoes are also making disease forecasting and control more difficult. According to Dr. Hoang Minh Duc, Director of the Department of Disease Prevention (Ministry of Health), despite Vietnam's implementation of numerous prevention and control measures, dengue fever remains an endemic infectious disease with a high number of cases. This reality necessitates continued evaluation of the effectiveness of existing solutions, updating scientific advancements, and selecting more appropriate disease prevention methods. The head of the Department of Disease Prevention emphasized the importance of combining international experience with Vietnam's practical conditions, strengthening control of disease-carrying mosquitoes, and raising public awareness to reduce the burden of disease. Get Health Tips From a treatment perspective, Professor Nguyen Van Kinh, former Director of the Central Hospital for Tropical Diseases, noted that dengue fever can become dangerous even after the patient's fever subsides. The period from the third to the seventh day of the illness is usually the time requiring special monitoring due to the risk of plasma leakage, shock, and serious complications. You may also like Therefore, patients should not be complacent when their fever subsides, but should monitor for warning signs and return for follow-up appointments as instructed. Children, the elderly, and those with underlying health conditions require special attention due to the increased risk of serious complications. Combine vaccination, mosquito control, and epidemiological surveillance. Find Vaccines Given the changing epidemic situation, experts believe that dengue prevention and control strategies need to shift strongly from responding to outbreaks to proactive prevention, with coordinated efforts between management agencies, the health system, and the community. One of the key topics of interest at the Dengue Fever Conference 2026 was the role of vaccination in the overall disease prevention strategy, alongside the mosquito control measures that have been implemented for many years. Dr. Rosana Richtmann, Director of the Infection Control Committee at Santa Joana Obstetrics and Gynecology Hospital and former President of the São Paulo Infectious Diseases Society ( Brazil ), shared her experience in implementing dengue fever vaccination in her country. Brazil began incorporating the vaccine into its public immunization program in 2024, initially in 521 localities, later expanding to 2,752 localities, with approximately 24 million doses distributed. Real-world data from São Paulo in 2024-2025 showed that those who received both doses had a 73.1% reduced risk of symptomatic dengue fever and an 87.9% reduced risk of hospitalization. This protective effect was observed even in the context of the re-emergence of the DENV-3 virus strain. Professor Nguyen Van Kinh presented the updated content in the draft Guidelines for the Diagnosis and Treatment of Dengue Fever in 2026. These results provide further evidence of the role of vaccination in reducing the burden of dengue fever. However, the protective efficacy depends on the target population, implementation conditions, and epidemiological characteristics, and therefore needs to be considered within the overall framework of disease prevention solutions. In Vietnam, Professor Nguyen Van Kinh presented updated content in the draft Guidelines for the Diagnosis and Treatment of Dengue Fever in 2026, which was developed based on the 2023 guidelines, incorporating new WHO recommendations and practical treatment experience. The draft focuses on updating the treatment of severe illnesses and complications, integrating guidelines for adults and children; it also adds bedside ultrasound techniques to support the assessment of shock, the risk of recurrent shock, and the selection of appropriate fluid resuscitation options. Notably, the preventive measures in the draft mention combining vaccination according to current regulations with epidemiological surveillance, mosquito bite prevention, larva and pupa eradication, and control of disease-transmitting insects. This approach aligns with the WHO's integrated dengue control guidelines, in which vaccination is one of the preventive tools, not a replacement for mosquito control measures and community involvement. You may also like Experts particularly emphasize the need for proactive disease prevention strategies, given that there is currently no specific antiviral treatment for dengue fever. Effective disease control requires the simultaneous implementation of multiple solutions, from monitoring cases and early detection of outbreaks to organizing timely treatment and raising public awareness of disease prevention. Mr. Benjamin Ping, General Director of Takeda Vietnam, stated that although community awareness of dengue fever is relatively high, many people still do not fully understand the risks and severity of the disease for themselves and their families. Therefore, strengthening evidence-based preventive measures, including appropriate vaccination, should be considered in the overall epidemic prevention and control strategy. At the community level, experts recommend that each family regularly clean their living environment, remove objects that collect water, cover household water containers tightly, eliminate mosquito larvae, and take measures to avoid mosquito bites. Mosquito spraying must follow professional guidelines to ensure effectiveness and safety. Regarding vaccinations, in a previous interview with reporters, doctors from the Long Chau Vaccination System stated that Qdenga is currently the first dengue fever vaccine licensed for circulation in Vietnam. This vaccine contains live attenuated dengue virus, capable of stimulating the body to create an immune response against all four types of dengue virus: DEN-1, DEN-2, DEN-3, and DEN-4. According to research data and practical implementation in many countries, after completing both doses, the vaccine is more than 80% effective in protecting against dengue fever after 12 months and helps reduce the risk of hospitalization by more than 90% after 18 months. Experts consider this a very important step in the fight against dengue fever, especially in the context of the disease becoming increasingly complex and showing an upward trend in many urban areas. Scale, impact and risk
